run --workspace .`\\n  Runs the next active\\\n  \\ or queued task once and leaves remaining work queued.\\n- `uv run litehive run\\\n  \\ --workspace . --dry-run`\\n  Shows the next planned task, selected engine, and\\\n  \\ predicted stop reason without invoking any agents.\\n- `uv run litehive run --workspace\\\n  \\ . --drain`\\n  Drains the live task pool until no active or queued task remains,\\\n  \\ re-reading queue state between tasks.\\n- `uv run litehive run --workspace . --drain\\\n  \\ --dry-run`\\n  Shows the planned pool order, selected engines, and predicted stop\\\n  \\ reason without invoking any agents.\\n- `scripts/run-all.sh .`\\n  Restarts `uv\\\n  \\ run litehive run` each iteration and writes timestamped logs under `.litehive/logs/run-all/`.\\n\\\n  - `scripts/run-all-status.sh .`\\n  Shows current `litehive status` plus the latest\\\n  \\ run-all log directory and recent output.\\n\\n## Engines\\n- Implemented adapters:\\\n  \\ `codex`, `opencode`, `gemini`, `copilot`.\\n- Planned later: `claude`.\\n- Engine\\\n  \\ selection precedence: run override, then task preference, then workspace default.\\n\\\n  \\n## Process decisions\\n- The intended execution loop is: `grooming -> implementing\\\n  \\ -> testing -> accepting -> commit_to_git`.\\n- QA rejection should return the same\\\n  \\ task to SWE; it should not become terminal failure.\\n- PM rejection should return\\\n  \\ the same task to SWE; it should not go back to backlog.\\n- Review rejection is\\\n  \\ normal iteration, not a final task outcome.\\n- `failed` is not the desired long-term\\\n  \\ lifecycle state for review rejection; state-machine cleanup is planned.\\n- Tasks\\\n  \\ that cannot be worked on right now should be parked or requeued, not dropped.\\n\\\n  - Explicit non-implementation outcomes such as `wont_do`, `deferred`, or `duplicate`\\\n  \\ should stay visible with rationale.\\n- Any future state-machine change should\\\n  \\ update the durable state-machine documentation in the repo.\\n- Workspace locking\\\n  \\ should become granular: short atomic locks for active-task transitions, but queue\\\n  \\ intake and non-conflicting queue updates should remain possible while a runner\\\n  \\ is active.\\n- Interrupted runners and subagents should become resumable states\\\n  \\ with recorded context rather than silent stale `running` state.\\n\\n## Development\\\n  \\ rules\\n- Keep changes scoped to the current task.\\n- Prefer targeted tests over\\\n  \\ broad test suites.\\n- Use local YAML state under `.litehive/` as the source of\\\n  \\ truth.\\n- Do not invent extra persistence layers unless the task requires it.\\n\\\n  - Prefer committed task outputs so completed changes are easy to revert and recover.\\n\\\n  \\n## Tool usage\\n- `codex` is currently used for self-hosted testing.\\n- `opencode`\\\n  \\ should run with model `zai-coding-plan/glm-5.1`.\\n- `gemini` and `copilot` adapters\\\n  \\ exist, but their usage should still be validated through real task runs.\\n- `claude`\\\n  \\ support is deferred and should remain opt-in because quota is limited.\\n- When\\\n  \\ claude support is added, prefer Claude Sonnet for testing rather than Opus to\\\n  \\ reduce quota usage.\\n- `opencode` must not inherit provider credential env vars;\\\n  \\ the adapter strips the same variables as the `oc()` wrapper from `~/.bashrc`.\\n\\\n  - Execution visibility is a first-class requirement: task runs should expose current\\\n  \\ stage, subagent status, transcript/output, and recent progress clearly.\\n- Long\\\n  \\ pool runs should leave durable per-iteration logs so failures can be diagnosed\\\n  \\ after the fact.\\n- Live subagent state should be written to disk while the subagent\\\n  \\ is still running, not only after completion.\\n- Tasks may opt into human checkpoints\\\n  \\ before acceptance or commit; those pauses should stop the pool cleanly and leave\\\n  \\ the task queued at the next stage.\\n- If you add a new workflow or command, document\\\n  \\ it here so future runs inherit the same context.\\n\\nShared process:\\n- Orchestrator\\\n  \\ model: the local runner is the manager and owns stage routing.\\n- Routing model:\\\n  \\ routing stays deterministic and local; subagents execute assigned stages but do\\\n  \\ not self-route.\\n- Shared stages: grooming -> implementing -> testing -> accepting\\\n  \\ -> commit_to_git.\\n- Role model: orchestrator-as-manager: `pm` grooms and accepts,\\\n  \\ `swe` implements, `qa` verifies.\\n- Source of truth: tasks and implementation\\\n  \\ state live under `.litehive/`.\\n- Task source of truth: issues or task records\\\n  \\ define scope; prompts and transcripts are supporting evidence.\\n- TDD expectations:\\\n  \\ prefer test-first or test-tight changes and explain deviations.\\n- Verification\\\n  \\ discipline: verification should be explicit, focused, and independent enough to\\\n  \\ catch regressions.\\n- Acceptance flow: implementation must pass verification before\\\n  \\ acceptance.\\n- Commit and recovery: successful tasks checkpoint to git; rollback\\\n  \\ and recover should remain deterministic.\\n\\nProject overlay:\\n- General software\\\n  \\ project workflow with deterministic local orchestration.\\n- Favor incremental,\\\n  \\ reviewable changes over broad refactors.\\n- Keep implementation, verification,\\\n  \\ and acceptance evidence explicit.\\n\\nPrompt scaffold:\\n- Start from the shared\\\n  \\ process contract, then add repository context and task data.\\n- Combine the generic\\\n  \\ base prompt with the selected project overlay instead of replacing the base.\\n\\\n  - Apply stage defaults first, then append any project-specific stage overlay for\\\n  \\ that step.\\n- Keep stage prompts explicit about role, verification expectations,\\\n  \\ and final report format.\\n\\nStage instructions:\\nClarify the task, inspect the\\\n  \\ repo if needed, and produce a concrete execution plan.\\nDo not make code changes\\\n  \\ in this stage.\\n\\nGoal:\\nRun task execution in dedicated git worktrees so task\\\n  \\ changes stay isolated from the main checkout. After acceptance, Litehive should\\\n  \\ own the final git integration flow: create the task commit in the worktree, merge\\\n  \\ or cherry-pick it back to main deterministically, and then clean up the worktree.\\n\\\n  \\nAcceptance criteria:\\n- Each runnable task can execute in its own isolated git\\\n  \\ worktree instead of the main checkout.\\n- Accepted work is committed by Litehive\\\n  \\ in the task worktree, not by the agent.\\n- Litehive merges, cherry-picks, or otherwise\\\n  \\ integrates the accepted task commit back to the main branch deterministically.\\n\\\n  - Main checkout stays clean except for the controlled integration step and persistent\\\n  \\ .litehive state.\\n\\nPlan:\\n- No plan defined.\\n\\nConstraints:\\n- Keep changes\\\n  \\ scoped to the task.\\n\\nReturn exactly this structure:\\nVERDICT: PASS|FAIL|REJECT|BLOCKED\\n\\\n  SUMMARY: one-line summary\\nFILES_CHANGED:\\n- path/to/file\\nTESTS_ADDED: <integer>\\n\\\n  TESTS_PASSING:
